Standard attention backward: Given Q, K, V, dO (dout), compute dQ, dK, dV. Standard attention forward: S = Q @ K^T * scale P = softmax(S) O = P @ V Backward: dV = P^T @ dO dP = dO @ V^T D = sum(dO * O, dim=-1) [or row sum of dO * O] dS = (dP - D) * P * scale dQ = dS @ K dK = dS^T @ Q For GQA: Q has shape [B, H, N, D] K, V have shape [B, H_kv, N, D] H = 32, H_kv = 8, so each K/V head is shared by 4 Q heads. Let's consider using `torch.matmul` for everything with a custom autograd-like manual backward. Step 1: scores = torch.matmul(q, k.transpose(-2, -1)) * scale # [B,H,N,N] Step 2: p = torch.softmax(scores, dim=-1) Step 3: o = torch.matmul(p, v) Step 4: d_v = torch.matmul(p.transpose(-2, -1), dout) # but need to handle GQA grouping Step 5: d_p = torch.matmul(dout, v.transpose(-2, -1)) Step 6: d_scores = p * (d_p - (dout * o).sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True)) Step 7: d_q = torch.matmul(d_scores, k) * scale Step 8: d_k = torch.matmul(d_scores.transpose(-2, -1), q) * scale Wait, step 6: The gradient of softmax is `p * (d_p - sum(p * d_p, dim=-1, keepdim=True))`. And sum(p * d_p) = sum(p * (dout @ v^T)) = sum(dout * (p @ v)) = sum(dout * o). So D = (dout * o).sum(dim=-1, keepdim=True). This matches! Then d_scores = p * (d_p - D). And d_q = d_scores @ k * scale. d_k = d_scores.transpose(-2,-1) @ q * scale. For GQA: In step 1, K and V are [B, H_kv, N, D]. We need to repeat them to match H. On A100/H100, `torch.matmul` with broadcasting handles this if we reshape appropriately. For example, Q: [B, H, N, D]. K: [B, H_kv, N, D]. scores = torch.matmul(q, k.transpose(-2,-1)) would broadcast K from H_kv to H if we use `unsqueeze`? Actually PyTorch matmul broadcasts batch dims. If q is [2,32,1024,128] and k.transpose is [2,8,128,1024], broadcasting rules: batch dims must be compatible. 32 and 8 are not compatible. So we need to explicitly repeat K/V. We can repeat K and V along the head dim: k_rep = k.repeat_interleave(H//H_kv, dim=1). Then k_rep is [B, H, N, D]. This uses memory (16MB -> 64MB). That's fine. Similarly for V. Then all matmuls are standard batched matmuls with B*H=64 batches. Because dQ[n,d] = sum_{m=0}^{Bc-1} dS[n,m] * K[m,d]. So for each m in 0..Bc-1, all threads need K[m, d0..d7] (good, broadcast). And each thread needs dS[n, m] for its rows n. But dS depends on n and m. If threads have different n, that's fine. However, if threads in a warp have different m (i.e., each thread handles a different set of m columns), then looping over all m would require iterating over the thread's own m columns, not a universal m. To make the loop universal, we need to reorganize: each thread should not be assigned fixed m columns for the dS storage. Instead, dS should be computed and stored in shared memory such that when we loop over m, each thread can read the dS value for its assigned n and the current m. But if each thread is assigned n0..n3 and d0..d7, and we loop over m from 0 to Bc-1, then thread needs dS[n0..n3, m]. Since m is the same for all threads in the warp, threads with the same n rows will read same addresses. Threads with different n rows will read different addresses. This is okay if smem is laid out row-major: dS[n, m] for fixed m and varying n is strided by Bc. If Bc is 64, then addresses are 64 apart. That might cause bank conflicts if the stride hits the same bank. Shared memory banks on H100/A100 are 32. Stride of 64 * 2 bytes = 128 bytes. 128 / 4 = 32 banks. So consecutive n access same bank? Wait, bank index = (addr / 4) % 32. For row-major smem_dp[n][m], address of [n][m] relative to row 0 is n * Bc * 2 + m * 2. For fixed m, addr_diff between n and n+1 is Bc*2 = 128 bytes. (128/4)%32 = 32%32 = 0. So all n map to the same bank for a given m! This is a severe bank conflict. To avoid this, we need to transpose the storage of dP and P, or use a swizzled layout. Instead of storing P as row-major [Br][Bc], store it as column-major [Bc][Br] or swizzled. If we store as column-major, then for fixed m, varying n are contiguous in memory (since column-major means elements vary fastest along n). Actually, column-major of size Br x Bc means memory layout is P[n][m] with n varying fast: addr = m*Br + n. For fixed m, varying n are contiguous. That's perfect for the dQ accumulation loop over m (where we read dS[n0..n3, m]). And for computing dP and P, we write to smem. Writing a tile with each thread handling a 4x4 sub-tile is easier if the layout is row major. But we can just write in the natural access pattern. Shared memory allocation: We can use a single `extern __shared__ char smem[];` and cast pointers. Or declare fixed-size arrays with `__shared__`. I'll define a helper to load a tile from global to shared memory. ``` template<int TILE_ROWS, int TILE_COLS, int COL_PAD> __device__ void load_tile(const __nv_bfloat16* gmem, int row_stride, int col_stride, int g_rows, int g_cols, int row_offset, __nv_bfloat16* smem, int base_tid, int num_threads) { // gmem shape: [g_rows, g_cols] with strides. // smem shape: [TILE_ROWS][COL_PAD] for (int idx = base_tid; idx < TILE_ROWS * TILE_COLS; idx += num_threads) { int r = idx / TILE_COLS; int c = idx % TILE_COLS; int g_r = row_offset + r; __nv_bfloat16 val = (g_r < g_rows && c < g_cols) ? gmem[g_r * row_stride + c * col_stride] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem[r * COL_PAD + c] = val; } } ``` But strides: Q/K/V/dO are row-major in global memory. Stride along N is `D`, along B/H is something else. We can compute pointer offsets in host code and pass base pointers for each head/tile. Code for warp reduce: ``` float row_max = local_max; row_max = fmaxf(row_max, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_max, 1)); row_max = fmaxf(row_max, __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_max, 2)); ``` With 4 threads, mask should be for the 4 threads. But we can just assume full warp and use `__shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 1)` and `__shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, val, 2)`. This works because threads outside the row will also participate, but they might belong to other rows. Wait, `__shfl_xor_sync` within a warp exchanges lanes. For row i, lanes are i*4, i*4+1, i*4+2, i*4+3. XOR with 1: lane ^ 1. For lane 0: swaps with 1. For lane 1: swaps with 0. For lane 2: swaps with 3. For lane 3: swaps with 2. So max across pairs (0,1) and (2,3). XOR with 2: lane ^ 2. Lane 0 <-> 2, Lane 1 <-> 3. So after both, max is across 0,1,2,3. But it also exchanges across other rows (e.g., lane 4<->6). However, since we only care about the threads in the same row, and the XOR pattern perfectly maps within the row's lanes, the shuffles from other rows go into our threads but we ignore them? No, `__shfl_xor_sync` returns the value from the source lane. For lane 0, XOR 2 gets value from lane 2 (same row). XOR 1 gets from lane 1 (same row). So it's fine. The operation is also done for lanes 4..7, etc., but they compute their own row's max. So a single warp shuffle with full warp mask works for all rows simultaneously. After row_max is known, each thread computes `expf(s_chunk[j] - row_max)` and `row_sum` locally, then warp shuffle sum similarly. Then, since only one thread per row should update the global max/sum, thread `i*4` (lane 0 of the row) can do: ``` if (lane_id % 4 == 0) { float old_max = max_reg[row]; float new_max = fmaxf(old_max, row_max); float exp_old = expf(old_max - new_max); float exp_new = row_sum * expf(row_max - new_max); max_reg[row] = new_max; sum_reg[row] = sum_reg[row] * exp_old + exp_new; } ``` Wait, all 4 threads have the same `row_max` and `row_sum` after shuffle. So we can have all threads compute the update identically. Then `max_reg[row]` is updated consistently. No need for a single thread. ``` float old_max = max_reg[row]; float new_max = fmaxf(old_max, row_max); sum_reg[row] = sum_reg[row] * expf(old_max - new_max) + row_sum * expf(row_max - new_max); max_reg[row] = new_max; ``` This works because all 4 threads in the row execute the same code with the same values. They all write to their own `max_reg` register (each thread has its own register copy). That's fine; they are per-thread registers. But we need the final max and sum after all tiles. The ds load is very lightweight (4 bf16s). The k load is 8 bf16s. If ds is strided, it's 4 loads, minor. Number of elements = Br * D = 8192. 256 threads -> 32 elements each (64 bytes -> 1 int4). So each thread loads 16x2bf16 or 32bf16? 32 bf16s = 64 bytes = `int4`. So each thread loads 32 bf16s. We can loop `e = tid; e < Br*D; e += blockDim.x*blockDim.y`. `int r = e / D; int c = e % D;` Load from global `Q[row_offset + r][c]` to `q_smem[r * D_PAD + c]`. Since threads with consecutive `e` load consecutive `c` for same `r`, they access consecutive global memory. Coalesced. For K tile loading to col-major shared memory: `k_smem[d * Bc + m] = K[m][d]`. We can similarly loop over all elements in the tile and write to transposed location. `e = tid; e < Bc*D; e += 256`. `int m = e / D; int d = e % D;` `k_smem[d * Bc + m] = global_K[m][d];` Here global access is row-major K[m][d], with d varying fastest. Consecutive `e` means consecutive `d` for fixed `m`. So global access is coalesced. This generic loading is simple. **Vectorization:** We can use `__nv_bfloat162` for q_smem and k_smem loads to halve the number of instructions. But for simplicity and correctness, let's use scalar loads first, and then add vectorized loads if needed. And use `torch.utils.cpp_extension.load_inline`. Let's structure the Python code: ```python import torch import torch.nn as nn from torch.utils.cpp_extension import load_inline cuda_source = r''' #include <torch/extension.h> #include <cuda_runtime.h> #include <cuda_fp16.h> #include <cuda_bf16.h> #include <math.h> // ... kernels ... void backward_cuda(... inputs ...) { // launch kernels } ''' cpp_source = ''' #include <torch/extension.h> // ... wrapper ... ''' module = load_inline( name="flash_attn_bwd_hopper", cpp_sources=cpp_source, cuda_sources=cuda_source, functions=["backward"], extra_cuda_cflags=["-O3", "--use_fast_math", "-gencode=arch=compute_90,code=sm_90"], verbose=True, ) class ModelNew(nn.Module): def __init__(self, num_heads, num_kv_heads, head_dim): super().__init__() self.num_heads = num_heads self.num_kv_heads = num_kv_heads self.head_dim = head_dim self.scale = 1.0 / (head_dim ** 0.5) def forward(self, q, k, v, dout): # q,k,v,dout are bf16 cuda tensors dq = torch.empty_like(q) dk = torch.empty_like(k) dv = torch.empty_like(v) # call module.backward(...) return dq, dk, dv ``` The C++ wrapper `backward` will take the tensors, allocate temporaries (m, l, D), and launch kernels. For `g_m`, `g_l`, `g_D`: shape [B, H, N]. Use `torch::empty`. Note: for GQA, stats (m,l,D) are per Q head, not per KV head. Let's write the C++ wrapper: ```cpp std::vector<torch::Tensor> backward( torch::Tensor q, torch::Tensor k, torch::Tensor v, torch::Tensor dout, int num_heads, int num_kv_heads, int head_dim ) { int B = q.size(0); int H = num_heads; int H_kv = num_kv_heads; int N = q.size(2); int D = head_dim; auto opts = torch::TensorOptions().dtype(torch::kFloat32).device(q.device()); auto m = torch::empty({B, H, N}, opts); auto l = torch::empty({B, H, N}, opts); auto D_stat = torch::empty({B, H, N}, opts); auto dq = torch::empty_like(q); auto dk = torch::empty_like(k); auto dv = torch::empty_like(v); const dim3 block_stats(256); // or whatever const dim3 grid_stats(B*H, (N + Br - 1)/Br); compute_stats_kernel<<<grid_stats, block_stats, smem_size_stats>>>(...); const dim3 block_dq(256); const dim3 grid_dq(B*H, (N + Br - 1)/Br); compute_dq_kernel<<<grid_dq, block_dq, smem_size_dq>>>(...); const dim3 block_dkv(256); const dim3 grid_dkv(B*H_kv, (N + Bc - 1)/Bc); compute_dk_dv_kernel<<<grid_dkv, block_dkv, smem_size_dkv>>>(...); return {dq, dk, dv}; } ``` Now, the kernel code. Let's write it carefully. I'll define tile sizes: ```cpp constexpr int Br = 64; constexpr int Bc = 64; constexpr int D_TILE = 128; // fixed constexpr int D_PAD = 132; constexpr int BR_PAD = 66; constexpr int BC = 64; // same as Bc ``` Wait, `Bc` and `BR_PAD` are different. Bc is the tile size (64). BR_PAD is the padding for the P matrix storage dimension (66). `smem_p` shape: [Bc][BR_PAD]. Let's write the dQ kernel. This is the most critical. ```cpp __global__ void compute_dq_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ k, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ v, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dout, const float* __restrict__ g_m, const float* __restrict__ g_l, const float* __restrict__ g_D, __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dq, int H, int H_kv, int N, int D, int sqb, int sqh, int sqn, int skb, int skh, int skn, int svb, int svh, int svn, int sdob, int sdoh, int sdon, int sdqb, int sdqh, int sdqn, float scale ) { const int b = blockIdx.x / H; const int h = blockIdx.x % H; const int kv_h = h / (H / H_kv); const int tile_i = blockIdx.y; const int row0 = tile_i * Br; if (row0 >= N) return; const int tid = threadIdx.x; // Shared memory declarations __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_q[Br][D_PAD]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_do[Br][D_PAD]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_k[D_PAD][Bc]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_v[D_PAD][Bc]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_p[Bc][BR_PAD]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_dp[Bc][BR_PAD]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_ds[Bc][BR_PAD]; __shared__ float smem_dq[Br][D_PAD]; // zero dq accumulator for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D_PAD; idx += blockDim.x) { smem_dq[idx / D_PAD][idx % D_PAD] = 0.0f; } // Load Q and dO tiles const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = q + b * sqb + h * sqh + row0 * sqn; const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dout + b * sdob + h * sdoh + row0 * sdon; for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / D; int c = idx % D; smem_q[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? q_base[r * sqn + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_do[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? do_base[r * sdon + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } // Load m, l, D for this tile into registers float m_reg[4]; float l_reg[4]; float D_reg[4]; // Each thread loads for its rows: we will map rows later. For now, load all into a small smem or just let all threads load everything and broadcast. // Since Br=64, and 256 threads, each thread loads one or two values. __shared__ float smem_m[Br]; __shared__ float smem_l[Br]; __shared__ float smem_D[Br]; for (int idx = tid; idx < Br; idx += blockDim.x) { if (row0 + idx < N) { smem_m[idx] = g_m[(b * H + h) * N + row0 + idx]; smem_l[idx] = g_l[(b * H + h) * N + row0 + idx]; smem_D[idx] = g_D[(b * H + h) * N + row0 + idx]; } } __syncthreads(); // Sub-tile assignment for S/dP: 16x16 grid -> each thread 4 rows x 4 cols // Sub-tile assignment for dQ: 16x16 grid -> each thread 4 rows x 8 cols // Wait, if we use 1D 256 threads, we can map: int tx = tid % 16; int ty = tid / 16; int rowA = ty * 4; // start row for S/dP and dQ int colA = tx * 4; // start col for S/dP // For dQ cols: each thread handles 8 cols. tx \in [0,15] -> colQ = tx * 8. const int NUM_TILES_KV = (N + Bc - 1) / Bc; for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < NUM_TILES_KV; ++tile_j) { int col0 = tile_j * Bc; if (col0 >= N) break; const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b * skb + kv_h * skh + col0 * skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_base = v + b * svb + kv_h * svh + col0 * svn; // Load K and V tiles (transposed into shared memory) for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int m = idx / D; int d = idx % D; smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m * skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_v[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? v_base[m * svn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); // Compute S tile (4x4 per thread) float s_acc[4][4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) s_acc[i][j] = 0.0f; for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) { float q_vals[4]; float k_vals[4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { q_vals[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_q[rowA + i][d]); } #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { k_vals[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][colA + j]); } #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { s_acc[i][j] += q_vals[i] * k_vals[j]; } } } // Convert to P and write to smem_p float p_reg[4][4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { float mi = smem_m[rowA + i]; float li = smem_l[rowA + i]; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { float p = expf(s_acc[i][j] * scale - mi) / li; p_reg[i][j] = p; smem_p[colA + j][rowA + i] = __float2bfloat16_rn(p); } } // Compute dP tile (4x4 per thread) float dp_acc[4][4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) dp_acc[i][j] = 0.0f; for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) { float do_vals[4]; float v_vals[4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { do_vals[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_do[rowA + i][d]); } #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { v_vals[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_v[d][colA + j]); } #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { dp_acc[i][j] += do_vals[i] * v_vals[j]; } } } // Compute dS and store to smem_ds #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { float Di = smem_D[rowA + i]; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { float ds = p_reg[i][j] * (dp_acc[i][j] - Di); smem_ds[colA + j][rowA + i] = __float2bfloat16_rn(ds); } } __syncthreads(); // Accumulate dQ: dQ += dS @ K // Each thread handles rows rowA..rowA+3, cols colQ..colQ+7 where colQ = tx*8 int colQ = tx * 8; for (int m = 0; m < Bc; ++m) { float ds_vals[4]; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { ds_vals[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_ds[m][rowA + i]); } float k_vals[8]; #pragma unroll for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) { k_vals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[colQ + dd][m]); // Wait! smem_k is [D_PAD][Bc]. So index is [d][m]. // colQ+dd is d dimension. m is the sequence dim. } #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { #pragma unroll for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) { smem_dq[rowA + i][colQ + dd] += ds_vals[i] * k_vals[dd]; } } } __syncthreads(); } // Write dQ to global memory __nv_bfloat16* dq_base = dq + b * sdqb + h * sdqh + row0 * sdqn; for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / D; int c = idx % D; if (row0 + r < N) { dq_base[r * sdqn + c] = __float2bfloat16_rn(smem_dq[r][c]); } } } ``` Wait, there's a bug: `smem_k` is `[D_PAD][Bc]`. Let's do that. // For V: dP = dO @ V^T, same access. v[d][m]. // For dV accumulation: dv[m][d] += P[i][m] * dO[i][d]. Access V? Actually we don't need V in the accumulation loop. Q should be row-major [Br][D_PAD]. // For p[i][m] access: we need varying m for fixed i. p_smem[m][i] (since [Bc][BR_PAD]). For fixed i, varying m: addr = m*BR_PAD+i. BR_PAD=66 -> bank increment 1. Good. const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + h_kv*skh + col0*skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_base = v + b*svb + h_kv*svh + col0*svn; for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int m = idx / D; int d = idx % D; smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_v[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? v_base[m*svn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } int rep = H / H_kv; for (int r = 0; r < rep; ++r) { int h_q = h_kv * rep + r; // iterate over Q tiles int NUM_TILES_Q = (N + Br - 1) / Br; for (int tile_i = 0; tile_i < NUM_TILES_Q; ++tile_i) { int row0 = tile_i * Br; // load Q and dO const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = q + b*sqb + h_q*sqh + row0*sqn; const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dout + b*sdob + h_q*sdoh + row0*sdon; for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int rr = idx / D; int c = idx % D; smem_q[rr][c] = (row0 + rr < N) ? q_base[rr*sqn + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_do[rr][c] = (row0 + rr < N) ? do_base[rr*sdon + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } // load stats __shared__ float smem_m[Br]; __shared__ float smem_l[Br]; __shared__ float smem_D[Br]; for (int idx = tid; idx < Br; idx += blockDim.x) { int rr = row0 + idx; int rr_clamped = min(rr, N-1); smem_m[idx] = g_m[(b*H + h_q)*N + rr_clamped]; smem_l[idx] = g_l[(b*H + h_q)*N + rr_clamped]; smem_D[idx] = g_D[(b*H + h_q)*N + rr_clamped]; } __syncthreads(); // compute S and dP tiles (same as dQ kernel but with Q,K swapped in sense? No, S = Q @ K^T same as before) // map threads: ty*4 rows, tx*4 cols. int tx = tid % 16; int ty = tid / 16; int rowA = ty * 4; int colA = tx * 4; // m dimension float s_acc[4][4]; // compute... for (int d=0; d<D; ++d) { ... } // Convert to P, write to smem_p // Compute dP = dO @ V^T float dp_acc[4][4]; // ... // Write dp to smem_dp __syncthreads(); // Accumulate dK and dV over i (rows) // Thread handles m cols colA..colA+3, d cols tx*8..tx*8+7. int colD = tx * 8; for (int i = 0; i < Br; ++i) { bool valid_i = (row0 + i < N); float Di = valid_i ? smem_D[i] : 0.0f; float p_vals[4]; float dp_vals[4]; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { p_vals[j] = valid_i ? __bfloat162float(smem_p[colA + j][i]) : 0.0f; dp_vals[j] = valid_i ? __bfloat162float(smem_dp[colA + j][i]) : 0.0f; } float ds_vals[4]; #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { ds_vals[j] = p_vals[j] * (dp_vals[j] - Di); } float do_vals[8]; float q_vals[8]; if (valid_i) { #pragma unroll for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) { do_vals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_do[i][colD + dd]); q_vals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_q[i][colD + dd]); } } else { #pragma unroll for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) { do_vals[dd] = 0.0f; q_vals[dd] = 0.0f; } } #pragma unroll for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) { #pragma unroll for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) { smem_dv[colA + j][colD + dd] += p_vals[j] * do_vals[dd]; smem_dk[colA + j][colD + dd] += ds_vals[j] * q_vals[dd]; } } } __syncthreads(); } } // Write dk and dv __nv_bfloat16* dk_base = dk + b*sdkb + h_kv*sdkh + col0*sdkn; __nv_bfloat16* dv_base = dv + b*svdb + h_kv*svdh + col0*svdn; for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int m = idx / D; int c = idx % D; if (col0 + m < N) { dk_base[m*sdkn + c] = __float2bfloat16_rn(smem_dk[m][c]); dv_base[m*svdn + c] = __float2bfloat16_rn(smem_dv[m][c]); } } } ``` Wait, `smem_dk` accumulates from multiple Q tiles and rep loops. But `smem_dk` and `smem_dv` sizes are `Bc * D_PAD * 4 = 64*132*4 = 33KB` each. Total 66KB. Fine. However, there is a critical issue: `smem_dk` and `smem_dv` are shared among all threads. The accumulation uses `smem_dk[colA + j][colD + dd] += ...`. Since different threads have different `colA` and `colD`, they write to different elements. No bank conflict if layout is row-major fp32 [Bc][D_PAD]. Bank width 4 bytes, which matches fp32. Consecutive `colD` are consecutive banks. Good. Threads with same `colA + j` but different `colD + dd` write to consecutive banks. No conflict. Perfect. One more thing: `smem_p` and `smem_dp` are written by threads during S/dP compute. In dK/dV accumulation, threads read `smem_p[colA + j][i]`. These read/write patterns are different. We have `__syncthreads()` after writes and before reads. Good. Now, the stats kernel. Let's write a clean version. Block: 256 threads. Grid: (B*H, (N + Br - 1) / Br). Each block handles a Br x N chunk of S (i.e., Br rows of Q, all N cols of K). SMEM: q[Br][D_PAD], k[Bc][D_PAD], v[Bc][D_PAD], do[Br][D_PAD]. Total ~67KB (with Bc=64). If Bc=128, total ~101KB. To get better occupancy, let's use Bc=64. Threads: 256. Mapping: 1D tid. Per-tile S compute: each thread computes a 1x1 element? No, we need to assign efficiently. Given the block computes max/sum per row, it can be simplified by having each warp handle one row, and threads in warp compute dot products with K columns. But K columns are 64 per tile. 32 threads per warp -> each thread computes 2 column dot products. Dot product over D=128: 4 lanes compute partial sum over 32 elements each, then warp shuffle add to get full dot. This is efficient. They need to reduce across the 4 lanes to get s. Then lane 0 writes s to shared memory? No, we need all 64 s values per row for softmax. Actually, we can compute 64 s values per row using warp-level parallelism over columns. Each warp handles 8 rows (since 32 threads). For each of the 8 rows, 32 threads compute dots with 32 K columns? Warp shuffle to find max over 32 values. Similarly sum. This maps well: `row = (threadIdx.x / 32) * 8 + ...` Wait. Let's assign `warp_id = threadIdx.x / 32` (0..7). `lane = threadIdx.x % 32`. That matches 4 lanes for D splitting. For each K column `m` (0..63): partial_s = dot(Q[row][lane*32 .. lane*32+31], K[m][lane*32 .. lane*32+31]); // shuffle reduce sum across lane 0..3 s = warp_shuffle_sum(partial_s); // but warp is 32 threads, includes 8 rows. // We need only the 4 threads for this row to reduce. Warp shuffle with XOR: `__shfl_xor_sync(mask, val, 1)` and `__shfl_xor_sync(mask, val, 2)`. Mask for the 4 threads: `0xF << (row % 8 * 4)`? This is messy because the 4 threads are not contiguous in the warp if row assignment is `threadIdx.x/4`. Actually, row = tid/4. For row 0: threads 0,1,2,3. Row 1: threads 4,5,6,7. ... Row 7: threads 28,29,30,31. So in warp 0, rows 0..7 have contiguous 4-thread groups. Great. So we can define `int row_in_warp = (threadIdx.x % 32) / 4;` and `int group_lane = threadIdx.x % 4;`. The mask for row `r` in warp is `0xF << (r*4)`. ``` unsigned int mask = 0xF << (row_in_warp * 4); s = partial_s; s += __shfl_xor_sync(mask, s, 1); s += __shfl_xor_sync(mask, s, 2); ``` Now `s` is the full dot product for thread's row and K column `m`, but only known to the 4 threads in the row group. We only need one thread per row to do the softmax update, say `group_lane == 0`. So for each m (0..63), after computing `s`, lane 0 updates the row's max and sum using online softmax. This leaves 3 threads idle for the softmax update, which is fine. We can have lane 0 maintain `row_max` and `row_sum` in registers. For each tile_j: Load K tile. Load V tile (for second pass). For m=0..63: compute s for row. if (group_lane == 0) { // update row_max, row_sum } End tile. After all tiles, lane 0 writes row_max, row_sum to g_m, g_l. Then second pass for D: lane 0 resets D=0. For each tile_j: Load K and V. For m=0..63: compute s. if (group_lane == 0) { p = expf(s*scale - row_max) / row_sum; // compute dot(do, v_m) // do_dot_v can be computed by all 4 threads in the row splitting D, then reducing. // Or have all 4 threads compute and reduce. } // We need do_dot_v = dot(dO_row, V_m). // dO_row is loaded in registers or smem. // V_m is in smem. 4 threads can compute partial dot. // partial_dov = dot(do_row[grp_lane*32 .. grp_lane*32+31], V_m[grp_lane*32 .. grp_lane*32+31]); // reduce across 4 threads with same shfl. // D += p * dov. End tiles. Write D. This is efficient and clean. We need to store Q and dO in shared memory? Or each thread can load its own Q and dO chunk into registers, or read from global. Since each row is handled by 4 threads, they share the same Q row. We can load Q row into shared memory so the 4 threads read from it. Or each thread reads from global into registers for its D chunk. Since each thread handles a D chunk of size 32, it can load 32 bf16s = 64 bytes (1 int4). Coalesced. In dot product loop over m (64 times), each thread reads its Q chunk and K/V chunk from shared memory or global. If K and V are in shared memory, great. For Q row: if we don't put it in shared memory, each of the 4 threads per row reads from global memory 64 times (once per m). That's 64 * 32 * 2 = 4KB per thread. 256 threads -> 1MB. Fine. But using shared memory for Q can reduce global traffic. Let's put Q tile in shared memory. Each row is read by 4 threads. smem_q[Br][D_PAD] = 64*132*2 = 16.9KB. Block 256 threads. Loading Q tile: each thread loads Br*D / 256 = 32 bf16s. Easy. For dO, also load into smem_do. So stats kernel SMEM: q, do, k, v. 4 * 16.9KB = 67.6KB. Actually, we can allocate: - smem_q: 16.9KB - smem_do: 16.9KB - smem_k: 16.9KB - smem_v: 16.9KB Total 67.6KB. First pass: load K only to smem_k. Second pass: load K to smem_k and V to smem_v. This is fine. Let's code the stats kernel with this structure. ```cpp __global__ void compute_stats_kernel( const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ q, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ k, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ v, const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dout, float* __restrict__ g_m, float* __restrict__ g_l, float* __restrict__ g_D, int H, int H_kv, int N, int D, int sqb, int sqh, int sqn, int skb, int skh, int skn, int svb, int svh, int svn, int sdob, int sdoh, int sdon, float scale ) { const int b = blockIdx.x / H; const int h = blockIdx.x % H; const int kv_h = h / (H / H_kv); const int tile_i = blockIdx.y; const int row0 = tile_i * Br_stats; // Br = 64 if (row0 >= N) return; const int tid = threadIdx.x; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_q[Br_stats][D_PAD]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_do[Br_stats][D_PAD]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_k[D_PAD][Bc_stats]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_v[D_PAD][Bc_stats]; // Load Q and dO tiles const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = q + b*sqb + h*sqh + row0*sqn; const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dout + b*sdob + h*sdoh + row0*sdon; for (int idx = tid; idx < Br_stats * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int r = idx / D; int c = idx % D; smem_q[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? q_base[r*sqn + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_do[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? do_base[r*sdon + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } const int NUM_TILES_KV = (N + Bc_stats - 1) / Bc_stats; // row and lane mapping int row = tid / 4; // 0..63 int lane = tid % 4; // 0..3 int row_global = row0 + row; bool valid_row = (row_global < N); // We split D=128 into 4 chunks of 32. int d_start = lane * 32; int d_end = d_start + 32; float row_max = -1e20f; float row_sum = 0.0f; // First pass: max and sum for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < NUM_TILES_KV; ++tile_j) { int col0 = tile_j * Bc_stats; const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + col0*skn; for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc_stats * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int m = idx / D; int d = idx % D; smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); // Each lane computes partial dot for columns m=0..Bc-1 for (int m = 0; m < Bc_stats; ++m) { if (!valid_row) break; // skip if invalid int g_col = col0 + m; if (g_col >= N) break; float partial_s = 0.0f; for (int d = d_start; d < d_end; ++d) { partial_s += __bfloat162float(smem_q[row][d]) * __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][m]); } // reduce across 4 lanes unsigned int mask = __activemask(); // or compute ahead // Better: since 4 threads per row are contiguous, mask for them is 0xF << ((tid % 32) / 4 * 4) unsigned int row_mask = 0xFU << (((tid % 32) / 4) * 4); float s = partial_s; s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 1); s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 2); if (lane == 0) { float old_max = row_max; float val = s * scale; row_max = fmaxf(old_max, val); row_sum = row_sum * expf(old_max - row_max) + expf(val - row_max); } } __syncthreads(); } if (lane == 0 && valid_row) { g_m[(b*H + h)*N + row_global] = row_max; g_l[(b*H + h)*N + row_global] = row_sum; } // Second pass: compute D float D_val = 0.0f; for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < NUM_TILES_KV; ++tile_j) { int col0 = tile_j * Bc_stats; const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + col0*skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_base = v + b*svb + kv_h*svh + col0*svn; for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc_stats * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int m = idx / D; int d = idx % D; smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_v[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? v_base[m*svn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); for (int m = 0; m < Bc_stats; ++m) { if (!valid_row) break; int g_col = col0 + m; if (g_col >= N) break; float partial_s = 0.0f; float partial_dov = 0.0f; for (int d = d_start; d < d_end; ++d) { float qd = __bfloat162float(smem_q[row][d]); float kd = __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][m]); float vd = __bfloat162float(smem_v[d][m]); float dod = __bfloat162float(smem_do[row][d]); partial_s += qd * kd; partial_dov += dod * vd; } unsigned int row_mask = 0xFU << (((tid % 32) / 4) * 4); float s = partial_s; s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 1); s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 2); float dov = partial_dov; dov += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, dov, 1); dov += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, dov, 2); if (lane == 0) { float p = expf(s * scale - row_max) / row_sum; D_val += p * dov; } } __syncthreads(); } if (lane == 0 && valid_row) { g_D[(b*H + h)*N + row_global] = D_val; } } ``` Note: `__activemask()` is dynamic and may not be correct if some threads diverge. Using explicit `row_mask` is better. `((tid % 32) / 4) * 4` gives the lane of the first thread in the row group (0,4,8,...,28). So `row_mask = 0xF << base_lane`. This is correct. However, `valid_row` check: if `row >= N - row0`, then the 4 lanes for that row are inactive. But within a warp, some rows might be active and some not. The `row_mask` assumes all 4 lanes in the group are active. If `row0 + row >= N`, then `valid_row` is false, and those lanes skip the loop. If all 4 lanes skip, they never call `__shfl_xor_sync`, which is fine. But if a warp has some valid and some invalid rows, the invalid rows don't execute shuffles. The valid rows call `__shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, ...)`. Since invalid lanes are not in the mask, it's fine if they don't participate. But the code path must be convergent up to the sync. Since we have a conditional `if (!valid_row) break;` inside the m loop, threads in the same warp diverge if some rows are valid and some are not. For invalid rows, they exit the loop and go to `__syncthreads()`. For valid rows, they continue. This divergence inside the warp is fine as long as they all eventually reach the same `__syncthreads()`. They do: valid threads finish the loop normally, invalid threads break early. Both reach the sync. So it's okay. But `__shfl_xor_sync` should be called by all threads in the mask. In the valid row group, all 4 threads are active and call it. The inactive threads in that row group (if any) are not. But since all 4 threads in a row are either all valid or all invalid (because row0 + row is same for all 4 threads sharing the row), it's fine. Wait, `row = tid / 4`. If `row0 + row >= N`, then all 4 threads for that row are invalid. So within a row group, all converge. But across rows in a warp, some groups execute the loop and some don't. That's warp-level divergence. Fine. Now, for the `compute_dq` and `compute_dk_dv` kernels, we need to make sure the 16x16 thread mapping has no divergence issues with row/col masking. Similar `valid_row` and `valid_col` checks. Let's check bank conflict for `smem_k` in stats kernel: `smem_k[d][m]` with varying d and fixed m. Access is `__bfloat162float(smem_k[d][m])` for d in `d_start..d_end`. This is sequential d for a single thread. Not across threads. So no bank conflict. Different threads access different m. But in the loop, all threads access their own m. Actually, the inner loop is over d for a fixed m. So all threads access different m simultaneously. For `smem_k` which is `[D_PAD][Bc]`, access pattern for thread: `addr = d * Bc + m`. As d increments, addr increments by Bc. Bc=64. 64*2=128 bytes. 128/4=32. Same bank. So a single thread iterating over d hits the same bank repeatedly. This is not a bank conflict (only one thread per bank). But it limits bank bandwidth per thread to 1 bank. Not ideal, but okay. If we had multiple threads in the warp iterating over d for different m, and if they happen to map to the same bank, there would be conflict. For `smem_k[d][m1]` and `smem_k[d][m2]`, addr diff = m2 - m1. Banks are 4 bytes. If m diff = 2, addr diff = 4 bytes -> next bank. So threads with different m that differ by 2 map to different banks. Fine. This is acceptable. Now, there's a small issue: `row_mask` computation uses `0xF << base_lane`. If `base_lane` is 28, then `0xF << 28` gives bits 28..31. Since unsigned int is 32-bit, `0xFU << 28` = `0xF0000000U`. This is fine. `__shfl_xor_sync` takes an unsigned 32-bit mask. Let's also think about `atan2` or other math functions? We only use expf and fmaxf. Let's double-check the dQ kernel for a potential bug: `smem_k` is declared as `__shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_k[D_PAD][Bc];`. In the dQ accumulation loop: `k_vals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[colQ + dd][m]);` Here `colQ + dd` indexes the first dimension `D_PAD` (0..127). `m` indexes the second dimension `Bc` (0..63). This is correct because `smem_k` stores transposed K. But wait, in the S compute for dQ kernel, K is accessed as: `k_vals[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][colA + j]);` This also matches. So `smem_k` is col-major `[D_PAD][Bc]`. Good. For V in dQ kernel: `v_vals[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_v[d][colA + j]);`. So `smem_v` is also `[D_PAD][Bc]`. In dQ kernel, `smem_v` is only used for dP compute. Good. In `compute_dk_dv` kernel, we also need `smem_q` and `smem_do` row-major `[Br][D_PAD]`. Access: `smem_q[i][colD + dd]`. Good. Wait, in `compute_dk_dv` kernel, for S compute: ``` float q_vals[4]; for (i=0;i<4;i++) q_vals[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_q[rowA + i][d]); float k_vals[4]; for (j=0;j<4;j++) k_vals[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][colA + j]); ``` This is correct. Now, padding. `D_PAD` should be large enough to avoid bank conflicts and hold D=128. Let's use `D_PAD = 132`. `BR_PAD = 66`. For `smem_q[Br][D_PAD]`: if `D_PAD=132`, `smem_q[r][c]` address = (r*132 + c)*2. For fixed c, varying r: diff = 264 bytes. bank diff = 66 mod 32 = 2. Okay. For `smem_k[D_PAD][Bc]`: `smem_k[d][m]` address = (d*Bc + m)*2. For fixed m, varying d: diff = Bc*2 = 128 bytes. bank diff = 32 mod 32 = 0. SAME BANK. Oh no! If a single thread iterates over d for fixed m, it hits the same bank. But since only one thread accesses that bank at a time for that m, it's not a conflict. However, if multiple threads iterate over d for different m, they might conflict if they map to the same bank. For threads in a warp computing S, they all have the same `d` but different `m`. `smem_k[d][m1]` vs `smem_k[d][m2]`: addr diff = (m2-m1)*2. If m diff = 2, addr diff = 4 bytes -> next bank. So threads with m offset 2 map to different banks. Since our 4 threads in a warp (for k_vals) have m = colA, colA+1, colA+2, colA+3. These map to banks b, b, b+1, b+1 (since bank width 4 bytes). So 2-way conflict. Not terrible. But to improve, we could pad Bc in the second dimension: `smem_k[D_PAD][Bc_PAD]`. Then addr = (d*Bc_PAD + m)*2. For fixed d, varying m: diff = 2 bytes -> 2-way conflict. Same as before. For fixed m, varying d: diff = Bc_PAD*2. If Bc_PAD=66, 132 bytes -> bank increment 1. Good for d-loop per thread. Let's use `Bc_PAD = 66` in `smem_k` and `smem_v`. But Bc is actual tile size (64). So allocate `[D_PAD][Bc_PAD]`. The extra columns are wasted but avoid bank issues. Similarly for `smem_p`, `smem_dp`, `smem_ds`: `[Bc][BR_PAD]`. We already have `BR_PAD=66`. Good. For `smem_q`, `smem_do`: `[Br][D_PAD]`. D_PAD=132. Good. For `smem_dq`: `[Br][D_PAD]` fp32. Address = (r*D_PAD + c)*4. For fixed r, varying c: diff = 4 bytes -> bank increment 1. Perfect. For fixed c, varying r: diff = D_PAD*4 = 528. 528/4=132. 132 mod 32 = 4. Bank increment 4. Fine. -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n__global__ void compute_stats_kernel(\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ q,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ k,\n sqb, int sqh, int sqn,\n int skb, int skh, int skn,\n int svb, int svh, int svn,\n int sdob, int sdoh, int sdon,\n float scale)\n{\n const int b = blockIdx.x / H;\n const int h = blockIdx.x % H;\n const int kv_h = h / (H / H_kv);\n const int tile_i = blockIdx.y;\n const int row0 = tile_i * Br;\n if (row0 >= N) return;\n const int tid = threadIdx.x;\n\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_q[Br][D_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_do[Br][D_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_k[D_PAD][BC_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_v[D_PAD][BC_PAD];\n\n // Load Q and dO tiles (row-major in global -> row-major in smem)\n const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = q + b*sqb + h*sqh + row0*sqn;\n const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dout + b*sdob + h*sdoh + row0*sdon;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int r = idx / D;\n int c = idx % D;\n smem_q[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? q_base[r*sqn + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n smem_do[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? do_base[r*sdon + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n\n const int NUM_TILES_KV = (N + Bc - 1) / Bc;\n int row = tid / 4; // which Q row within tile (0..63)\n int lane = tid % 4; // which D-chunk (0..3)\n int row_global = row0 + row;\n bool valid_row = (row_global < N);\n int d_start = lane * 32;\n int d_end = d_start + 32;\n\n float row_max = -1e30f;\n float row_sum = 0.0f;\n\n // first pass: row-wise online softmax\n for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < NUM_TILES_KV; ++tile_j) {\n int col0 = tile_j * Bc;\n const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + col0*skn;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int m = idx / D;\n int d = idx % D;\n smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n __syncthreads();\n for (int m = 0; m < Bc; ++m) {\n if (!valid_row) break;\n int g_col = col0 + m;\n if (g_col >= N) break;\n float partial_s = 0.0f;\n #pragma unroll\n for (int d = d_start; d < d_end; ++d) {\n partial_s += __bfloat162float(smem_q[row][d]) * __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][m]);\n }\n int base_lane = ((tid % 32) / 4) * 4;\n unsigned int row_mask = 0xFU << base_lane;\n float s = partial_s;\n s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 1);\n s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 2);\n if (lane == 0) {\n float val = s * scale;\n float old_max = row_max;\n row_max = fmaxf(old_max, val);\n row_sum = row_sum * expf(old_max - row_max) + expf(val - row_max);\n }\n }\n __syncthreads();\n }\n if (lane == 0 && valid_row) {\n g_m[(b*H + h)*N + row_global] = row_max;\n g_l[(b*H + h)*N + row_global] = row_sum;\n }\n\n // second pass: compute D_i = sum_m P_im * dot(dO_i, V_m)\n float D_val = 0.0f;\n for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < NUM_TILES_KV; ++tile_j) {\n int col0 = tile_j * Bc;\n const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + col0*skn;\n const __nv_bfloat16* v_base = v + b*svb + kv_h*svh + col0*svn;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int m = idx / D;\n int d = idx % D;\n smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n smem_v[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? v_base[m*svn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n __syncthreads();\n for (int m = 0; m < Bc; ++m) {\n if (!valid_row) break;\n int g_col = col0 + m;\n if (g_col >= N) break;\n float partial_s = 0.0f;\n float partial_dov = 0.0f;\n #pragma unroll\n for (int d = d_start; d < d_end; ++d) {\n float qv = __bfloat162float(smem_q[row][d]);\n float kv = __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][m]);\n float vv = __bfloat162float(smem_v[d][m]);\n float dov = __bfloat162float(smem_do[row][d]);\n partial_s += qv * kv;\n partial_dov += dov * vv;\n }\n int base_lane = ((tid % 32) / 4) * 4;\n unsigned int row_mask = 0xFU << base_lane;\n float s = partial_s;\n s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 1);\n s += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, s, 2);\n float dov = partial_dov;\n dov += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, dov, 1);\n dov += __shfl_xor_sync(row_mask, dov, 2);\n if (lane == 0) {\n float p = expf(s * scale - row_max) / row_sum;\n D_val += p * dov;\n }\n }\n __syncthreads();\n }\n if (lane == 0 && valid_row) {\n g_D[(b*H + h)*N + row_global] = D_val;\n }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Kernel 2: compute dQ\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n__global__ void compute_dq_kernel(\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ q,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ k,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ v,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dout,\n const float* __restrict__ g_m,\n const float* __restrict__ g_l,\n const float* __restrict__ g_D,\n __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dq,\n int H, int H_kv, int N, int D,\n int sqb, int sqh, int sqn,\n int skb, int skh, int skn,\n int svb, int svh, int svn,\n int sdob, int sdoh, int sdon,\n int sdqb, int sdqh, int sdqn,\n float scale)\n{\n const int b = blockIdx.x / H;\n const int h = blockIdx.x % H;\n const int kv_h = h / (H / H_kv);\n const int tile_i = blockIdx.y;\n const int row0 = tile_i * Br;\n if (row0 >= N) return;\n const int tid = threadIdx.x;\n const int tx = tid % 16;\n const int ty = tid / 16;\n const int rowA = ty * 4;\n const int colA = tx * 4;\n const int colQ = tx * 8;\n\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_q[Br][D_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_do[Br][D_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_k[D_PAD][BC_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_v[D_PAD][BC_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_p[Bc][BR_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_dp[Bc][BR_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_ds[Bc][BR_PAD];\n __shared__ float smem_dq[Br][D_PAD];\n\n // zero accumulator\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D_PAD; idx += blockDim.x) {\n smem_dq[idx / D_PAD][idx % D_PAD] = 0.0f;\n }\n\n // load Q and dO\n const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = q + b*sqb + h*sqh + row0*sqn;\n const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dout + b*sdob + h*sdoh + row0*sdon;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int r = idx / D;\n int c = idx % D;\n smem_q[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? q_base[r*sqn + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n smem_do[r][c] = (row0 + r < N) ? do_base[r*sdon + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n\n // load stats for tile\n __shared__ float smem_m[Br];\n __shared__ float smem_l[Br];\n __shared__ float smem_D[Br];\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int rr = row0 + idx;\n int rr_c = min(rr, N - 1);\n smem_m[idx] = g_m[(b*H + h)*N + rr_c];\n smem_l[idx] = g_l[(b*H + h)*N + rr_c];\n smem_D[idx] = g_D[(b*H + h)*N + rr_c];\n }\n __syncthreads();\n\n const int NUM_TILES_KV = (N + Bc - 1) / Bc;\n for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < NUM_TILES_KV; ++tile_j) {\n int col0 = tile_j * Bc;\n if (col0 >= N) break;\n const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + col0*skn;\n const __nv_bfloat16* v_base = v + b*svb + kv_h*svh + col0*svn;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int m = idx / D;\n int d = idx % D;\n smem_k[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n smem_v[d][m] = (col0 + m < N) ? v_base[m*svn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n __syncthreads();\n\n // compute S tile (4x4 per thread)\n float s_acc[4][4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j)\n s_acc[i][j] = 0.0f;\n\n for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) {\n float qv[4];\n float kv[4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) qv[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_q[rowA + i][d]);\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) kv[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[d][colA + j]);\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j)\n s_acc[i][j] += qv[i] * kv[j];\n }\n\n // softmax to P and write\n float p_reg[4][4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {\n bool vrow = (row0 + rowA + i < N);\n float mi = smem_m[rowA + i];\n float li = smem_l[rowA + i];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) {\n bool vcol = (col0 + colA + j < N);\n float p = 0.0f;\n if (vrow && vcol) p = expf(s_acc[i][j] * scale - mi) / li;\n p_reg[i][j] = p;\n smem_p[colA + j][rowA + i] = __float2bfloat16_rn(p);\n }\n }\n\n // compute dP tile (4x4 per thread)\n float dp_acc[4][4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j)\n dp_acc[i][j] = 0.0f;\n\n for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) {\n float dov[4];\n float vv[4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) dov[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_do[rowA + i][d]);\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) vv[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_v[d][colA + j]);\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j)\n dp_acc[i][j] += dov[i] * vv[j];\n }\n\n // compute dS and write to smem_ds\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {\n float Di = smem_D[rowA + i];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) {\n float ds = p_reg[i][j] * (dp_acc[i][j] - Di);\n smem_ds[colA + j][rowA + i] = __float2bfloat16_rn(ds);\n }\n }\n __syncthreads();\n\n // accumulate dQ: dQ += dS @ K\n for (int m = 0; m < Bc; ++m) {\n float ds_vals[4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n ds_vals[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_ds[m][rowA + i]);\n float kvals[8];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd)\n kvals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[colQ + dd][m]);\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n #pragma unroll\n for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd)\n smem_dq[rowA + i][colQ + dd] += ds_vals[i] * kvals[dd];\n }\n __syncthreads();\n }\n\n // write out dQ\n __nv_bfloat16* dq_base = dq + b*sdqb + h*sdqh + row0*sdqn;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int r = idx / D;\n int c = idx % D;\n if (row0 + r < N)\n dq_base[r*sdqn + c] = __float2bfloat16_rn(smem_dq[r][c]);\n }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Kernel 3: compute dK and dV (fused, accumulates over all Q heads for GQA)\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n__global__ void compute_dk_dv_kernel(\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ q,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ k,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ v,\n const __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dout,\n const float* __restrict__ g_m,\n const float* __restrict__ g_l,\n const float* __restrict__ g_D,\n __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dk,\n __nv_bfloat16* __restrict__ dv,\n int H, int H_kv, int N, int D,\n int sqb, int sqh, int sqn,\n int skb, int skh, int skn,\n int svb, int svh, int svn,\n int sdob, int sdoh, int sdon,\n int sdkb, int sdkh, int sdkn,\n int svdb, int svdh, int svdn,\n float scale)\n{\n const int b = blockIdx.x / H_kv;\n const int h_kv = blockIdx.x % H_kv;\n const int tile_j = blockIdx.y;\n const int col0 = tile_j * Bc;\n if (col0 >= N) return;\n const int tid = threadIdx.x;\n const int tx = tid % 16;\n const int ty = tid / 16;\n const int rowA = ty * 4; // Q-rows handled for S/dP\n const int colA = tx * 4; // K/V-cols handled for S/dP\n const int colD = tx * 8; // D-cols handled for dk/dv accum\n\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_k[BC_PAD][D_PAD]; // row-major for K tile (loaded straight)\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_v[BC_PAD][D_PAD]; // row-major for V tile\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_q[Br][D_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_do[Br][D_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_p[Bc][BR_PAD];\n __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_dp[Bc][BR_PAD];\n __shared__ float smem_dk[Bc][D_PAD];\n __shared__ float smem_dv[Bc][D_PAD];\n\n // zero dk/dv accumulators\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D_PAD; idx += blockDim.x) {\n smem_dk[idx / D_PAD][idx % D_PAD] = 0.0f;\n smem_dv[idx / D_PAD][idx % D_PAD] = 0.0f;\n }\n\n // load K and V tiles once\n const __nv_bfloat16* k_base = k + b*skb + h_kv*skh + col0*skn;\n const __nv_bfloat16* v_base = v + b*svb + h_kv*svh + col0*svn;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int m = idx / D;\n int d = idx % D;\n smem_k[m][d] = (col0 + m < N) ? k_base[m*skn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n smem_v[m][d] = (col0 + m < N) ? v_base[m*svn + d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n\n int rep = H / H_kv;\n const int NUM_TILES_Q = (N + Br - 1) / Br;\n\n for (int r = 0; r < rep; ++r) {\n int h_q = h_kv * rep + r;\n for (int tile_i = 0; tile_i < NUM_TILES_Q; ++tile_i) {\n int row0q = tile_i * Br;\n // load Q and dO for this Q head\n const __nv_bfloat16* q_base = q + b*sqb + h_q*sqh + row0q*sqn;\n const __nv_bfloat16* do_base = dout + b*sdob + h_q*sdoh + row0q*sdon;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int rr = idx / D;\n int c = idx % D;\n smem_q[rr][c] = (row0q + rr < N) ? q_base[rr*sqn + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n smem_do[rr][c] = (row0q + rr < N) ? do_base[rr*sdon + c] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f);\n }\n // load stats\n __shared__ float smem_m[Br];\n __shared__ float smem_l[Br];\n __shared__ float smem_Dstat[Br];\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Br; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int rr = row0q + idx;\n int rr_c = min(rr, N - 1);\n smem_m[idx] = g_m[(b*H + h_q)*N + rr_c];\n smem_l[idx] = g_l[(b*H + h_q)*N + rr_c];\n smem_Dstat[idx] = g_D[(b*H + h_q)*N + rr_c];\n }\n __syncthreads();\n\n // compute S tile (Q @ K^T) and dP tile (dO @ V^T)\n float s_acc[4][4];\n float dp_acc[4][4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i)\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) {\n s_acc[i][j] = 0.0f;\n dp_acc[i][j] = 0.0f;\n }\n\n for (int d = 0; d < D; ++d) {\n float qv[4], dov[4], kk[4], vv[4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {\n qv[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_q[rowA + i][d]);\n dov[i] = __bfloat162float(smem_do[rowA + i][d]);\n }\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) {\n kk[j] = __bfloat162float(smem_k[colA + qv[i] * kk[j];\n dp_acc[i][j] += dov[i] * vv[j];\n }\n }\n\n // write P and dP to smem\n #pragma unroll\n for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {\n bool vrow = (row0q + rowA + i < N);\n float mi = smem_m[rowA + li;\n smem_p[colA + j][rowA + i] = __float2bfloat16_rn(p);\n smem_dp[colA + j][rowA + i] = __float2bfloat16_rn(dp_acc[i][j]);\n }\n }\n __syncthreads();\n\n // accumulate dK and dV over i (Q rows)\n for (int i = 0; i < Br; ++i) {\n bool valid_i = (row0q + i < N);\n float Di = valid_i ? smem_Dstat[i] : 0.0f;\n float pvals[4], dpvals[4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j) {\n pvals[j] = valid_i ? __bfloat162float(smem_p[colA + j][i]) : 0.0f;\n dpvals[j] = valid_i ? __bfloat162float(smem_dp[colA + j][i]) : 0.0f;\n }\n float dsvals[4];\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j)\n dsvals[j] = pvals[j] * (dpvals[j] - Di);\n\n float dovals[8], qvals[8];\n if (valid_i) {\n #pragma unroll\n for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) {\n dovals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_do[i][colD + dd]);\n qvals[dd] = __bfloat162float(smem_q[i][colD + dd]);\n }\n } else {\n #pragma unroll\n for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) {\n dovals[dd] = 0.0f;\n qvals[dd] = 0.0f;\n }\n }\n #pragma unroll\n for (int j = 0; j < 4; ++j)\n #pragma unroll\n for (int dd = 0; dd < 8; ++dd) {\n smem_dv[colA + j][colD + dd] += pvals[j] * dovals[dd];\n smem_dk[colA + j][colD + dd] += dsvals[j] * qvals[dd];\n }\n }\n __syncthreads();\n }\n }\n\n // write dk, dv\n __nv_bfloat16* dk_base = dk + b*sdkb + h_kv*sdkh + col0*sdkn;\n __nv_bfloat16* dv_base = dv + b*svdb + h_kv*svdh + col0*svdn;\n for (int idx = tid; idx < Bc * D; idx += blockDim.x) {\n int m = idx / D;\n int c = idx % D;\n if (col0 + m < N) {\n dk_base[m*sdkn + c] = __float2bfloat16_rn(smem_dk[m][c]);\n dv_base[m*svdn + c] = __float2bfloat16_rn(smem_dv[m][c]);\n }\n }\n}\n\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\n// Host launcher\n// -----------------------------------------------------------------------------\nstd::vector<torch::Tensor> backward(\n torch::Tensor q, torch::Tensor k, torch::Tensor v, torch::Tensor dout,\n int num_heads, int num_kv_heads, int head_dim)\n{\n int B = q.size(0);\n int N = q.size(2);\n int D = head_dim;\n int H = num_heads;\n int H_kv = num_kv_heads;\n float scale = 1.0f / sqrtf((float)D);\n\n auto opts_f32 = torch::TensorOptions().dtype(torch::kFloat32).device(q.device());\n auto m = torch::empty({B, H, N}, opts_f32);\n auto l = torch::empty({B, H, N}, opts_f32);\n auto D_stat = torch::empty({B, H, N}, opts_f32);\n\n auto dq = torch::empty_like(q);\n auto dk = torch::empty_like(k);\n auto dv = torch::empty_like(v);\n\n auto q_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(q.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto k_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(k.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto v_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(v.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto do_ptr = reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(dout.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto dq_ptr = reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(dq.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto dk_ptr = reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(dk.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto dv_ptr = reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16*>(dv.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>());\n auto m_ptr = m.data_ptr<float>();\n auto l_ptr = l.data_ptr<float>();\n auto D_ptr = D_stat.data_ptr<float>();\n\n int sqb = For dK and dV: - Similarly, one warp per (b, h_kv, j) - But for GQA, each KV head is shared by rep = H/H_kv query heads - So each warp needs to iterate over all rep*Q_heads for that KV head and accumulate - Grid: (B * H_kv, N) blocks, each with 32 threads Let me write this warp-level approach. It will be correct and memory-bound but avoid all shared memory issues. **Warp-level dQ kernel:** Each warp handles one (b, h, i): 1. Load Q[i,d] and dO[i,d] into registers (4 bf16 per thread for D=128/32=4) 2. For j in 0..N-1: a. Load K[j,d] and V[j,d] (4 bf16 per thread) b. Compute S_ij = sum_d Q[i,d]*K[j,d] via warp shuffle c. After warp reduction, broadcast S_ij to all threads d. Compute P_ij = exp(S_ij * scale - m_i) / l_i e. Compute dP_ij = sum_d dO[i,d]*V[j,d] via warp shuffle f. Compute dS_ij = P_ij * (dP_ij - D_i) g. Accumulate dQ[i,d] += dS_ij * K[j,d] 3. Write dQ[i,d] Memory access pattern: Each warp reads Q[i,:] and dO[i,:] once. Then iterates j, reading K[j,:] and V[j,:]. This is sequential for j, but across warps it's parallel. Since K and V are read for all i, there's no reuse at the warp level, but across SMs the L2 cache will help. **Warp-level dK kernel:** Each warp handles one (b, h_kv, j): 1. Load K[j,d] into registers (but we'll compute dK[j,d]) 2. For each query head h_q in h_kv's group (h_kv * rep to h_kv * rep + rep - 1): a. For i in 0..N-1: i. Load Q[i,d], dO[i,d], m[i], l[i], D[i] ii. Compute S_ij, P_ij, dP_ij iii. dS_ij = P_ij * (dP_ij - D_i) iv. Accumulate dK[j,d] += dS_ij * Q[i,d] v. Accumulate dV[j,d] += P_ij * dO[i,d] Actually for efficiency, I can fuse dK and dV in one kernel per warp: Each warp computes both dK[j,:] and dV[j,:] by iterating over all relevant query heads and all i. For GQA with rep=4: For each of the 4 query heads: For each i from 0 to N-1: Load stats for (b, h_q, i) Compute S_ij, P_ij, dP_ij, dS_ij Accumulate dK[j,:] += dS_ij * Q_hq[i,:] Accumulate dV[j,:] += P_ij * dO_hq[i,:] This is O(rep * N * D) per warp. With B=2, H_kv=8, N=1024, we have 2*8*1024 = 16384 warps. Each warp does 4*1024 iterations = 4096 steps. Total work = 16384 * 4096 * 128 = 8.6B ops, which is fine. But register usage per thread: Q[i,d] (4), dO[i,d] (4), K[j,d] (4), V[j,d] (4), accum_dK (4), accum_dV (4) = 24 bf16s = 48 bytes. Plus a few floats. This is well within 255 registers. Wait, the dQ kernel computes S_ij and dP_ij which each require a reduction across the warp. Let me make sure the warp shuffle works correctly. Each thread holds 4 dimensions. To compute dot(Q[i], K[j]): - Each thread computes partial = sum of its 4 Q[i,d] * K[j,d] - Then warp reduce using __shfl_xor_sync With 32 threads in a warp: ``` float partial = 0; for (int d_local = 0; d_local < 4; ++d_local) { partial += float(Q[i][d_local]) * float(K[j][d_local]); } // warp reduction for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { partial += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, partial, offset); } float S_ij = partial * scale; ``` For the dQ kernel, stats m[i], l[i], D[i] are precomputed. So we can store them in shared memory or read them from global memory. Since each warp handles one i, all threads in the warp need the same m[i], l[i], D[i]. We can have thread 0 read them and broadcast via __shfl_sync. Actually, to avoid shared memory entirely, just have thread 0 load m[i], l[i], D[i] and broadcast: `float m_i = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, m_ptr[...], 0);` The only issue is coalescing. For a warp reading Q[i,:], if thread t reads Q[i, t*4 + 0..3], that's contiguous and coalesced. Similarly for K[j,:]. For iterating j from 0 to N-1, consecutive warps access the same j but different i. Warps within a block that access the same j might have some locality via L1/L2. Actually, I should increase the work per block to amortize the j-iteration overhead. Instead of 1 warp per block, use 8 warps per block, each handling different i values. Then they all iterate j together, accessing K[j,:] and V[j,:] simultaneously. This is much more L1-friendly. So block size = 8 warps = 256 threads. Each block handles 8 different i values (1 per warp). They loop j in sync, loading K[j,:] and V[j,:] into shared memory once per j. This is the sweet spot: use a little shared memory for K/V to amortize loads across warps. Let me redesign with small shared memory for K/V: **dQ kernel with K/V in shared memory:** - Block: 256 threads (8 warps) - Each block handles 8 rows (i values), 1 per warp - The 32 threads in each warp handle D=128 dimensions (4 per thread) - Shared memory per j: K[j,0:127] and V[j,0:127] stored as bf16 - Wait, if we're iterating j sequentially, we could load a tile of K and V - For Bc = 32: smem_K[32][132] ~ 4.2KB, smem_V[32][132] ~ 4.2KB. Total ~8.4KB Design: - Each block handles 8 rows (warp 0 -> i0, warp 1 -> i1, etc.) - Loop over j in tiles of Bc=32 - Load tile K[j0:j0+31, :] and V[j0:j0+31, :] into shared memory (coalesced, all threads participate) - Each warp then iterates m=0..31 - Compute S_i(j0+m) using Q_i (in registers) and K[j0+m,:] (shared memory) - Compute dP similarly with V - Update dQ accumulator For 8 rows and Bc=32: - smem_K[32][132] ~ 4224 bytes - smem_V[32][132] ~ 4224 bytes Total 8448 bytes ✓ well under 48KB For the dq accumulators, keep them in registers (8 warps * 32 threads/warp * 4 floats = 1024 floats = 4KB total). The only extra is precomputed stats m, l, D for the 8 i values. - smem_m[8], smem_l[8], smem_D[8] = negligible So my revised plan: 1. Let me draft the kernels: **Constants:** ```cpp constexpr int D = 128; constexpr int D_PER_THREAD = 4; // 128 / 32 threads constexpr int WARP_SIZE = 32; constexpr int ROWS_PER_BLOCK = 8; // for stats and dq constexpr int BC = 32; // KV tile size constexpr int BR_DKV = 32; // Q tile size for dk/dv constexpr int BC_DKV = 8; // KV rows per block for dk/dv ``` **compute_stats_kernel:** ```cpp __global__ void compute_stats_kernel(...) { int b = blockIdx.x / H; int h = blockIdx.x % H; int kv_h = h / (H / H_kv); int row0 = blockIdx.y * ROWS_PER_BLOCK; int tid = threadIdx.x; int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int row = row0 + warp_id; if (row >= N) return; // warp is idle if (warp_id >= ROWS_PER_BLOCK) return; // Load Q and dO for this row into registers const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = q + b*sqb + h*sqh + row*sqn; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = dout + b*sdob + h*sdoh + row*sdon; float q_reg[4], do_reg[4]; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; q_reg[i] = (d < D) ? __bfloat162float(q_ptr[d]) : 0.0f; do_reg[i] = (d < D) ? __bfloat162float(do_ptr[d]) : 0.0f; } float row_max = -1e30f; float row_sum = 0.0f; float D_val = 0.0f; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_K[BC][D+4]; // pad to 132 __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_V[BC][D+4]; // First pass: compute m and l for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < (N + BC - 1) / BC; ++tile_j) { int j0 = tile_j * BC; // Load K and V tile collaboratively for (int idx = tid; idx < BC * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int jj = idx / D; int d = idx % D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + (j0 + jj)*skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = v + b*svb + kv_h*svh + (j0 + jj)*svn; bool valid = (j0 + jj < N); smem_K[jj][d] = valid ? k_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_V[jj][d] = valid ? v_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); for (int jj = 0; jj < BC; ++jj) { int j = j0 + jj; if (j >= N) break; float s_acc = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { s_acc += q_reg[i] * __bfloat162float(smem_K[jj][lane * 4 + i]); } // Warp reduce #pragma unroll for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { s_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, s_acc, offset); } if (lane == 0) { float val = s_acc * scale; float old_m = row_max; row_max = fmaxf(old_m, val); row_sum = row_sum * expf(old_m - row_max) + expf(val - row_max); } } __syncthreads(); } // Second pass: compute D row_max = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_max, 0); row_sum = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_sum, 0); for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < (N + BC - 1) / BC; ++tile_j) { int j0 = tile_j * BC; // We could skip loading K again if we don't need it, but we need V for (int idx = tid; idx < BC * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int jj = idx / D; int d = idx % D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + (j0 + jj)*skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = v + b*svb + kv_h*svh + (j0 + jj)*svn; bool valid = (j0 + jj < N); smem_K[jj][d] = valid ? k_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_V[jj][d] = valid ? v_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); for (int jj = 0; jj < BC; ++jj) { int j = j0 + jj; if (j >= N) break; float s_acc = 0.0f, dov_acc = 0.0f; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; s_acc += q_reg[i] * __bfloat162float(smem_K[jj][d]); dov_acc += do_reg[i] * __bfloat162float(smem_V[jj][d]); } #pragma unroll for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { s_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, s_acc, offset); dov_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, dov_acc, offset); } if (lane == 0) { float p = expf(s_acc * scale - row_max) / row_sum; D_val += p * dov_acc; } } __syncthreads(); } if (lane == 0) { int idx = (b * H + h) * N + row; m_ptr[idx] = row_max; l_ptr[idx] = row_sum; D_ptr[idx] = D_val; } } ``` Wait, there's a subtle bug. In the second pass, I'm loading K again but I don't actually need K for D computation... I need S_ij to compute P_ij. Yes I do need K for S_ij. So loading both K and V is correct. Actually, there's a potential synchronization issue: `__syncthreads()` inside a conditional `if (row >= N) return;` can cause deadlock because some warps might return early. I should handle this by letting all threads participate in the __syncthreads but only doing the computation conditionally. In my design, each block has 8 warps. If row0 is near N, some warps might have row >= N. For those warps, I should still participate in __syncthreads and shared memory loads, but skip the computation. Let me restructure: ```cpp bool active = (row < N); ``` And only do the computation inside `if (active)` blocks, but all threads participate in `__syncthreads()` and shared memory writes. Actually, for the shared memory loads, all threads should participate regardless. For the loop over jj, active warps do the computation and `__shfl_xor_sync`. For inactive warps, they still participate in shuffles (but with 0 values) and should be fine. Wait, `__shfl_xor_sync` requires all threads in the mask to be converged. If an inactive warp calls `__shfl_xor_sync` it should still work (the warp is convergent within itself). But the `__syncthreads()` is the critical part. I need to ensure all threads in the block reach every `__syncthreads()`. So I'll structure it as: ```cpp for (int tile_j = 0; ...; ++tile_j) { // All threads load smem for (int idx = tid; ...) { ... } __syncthreads(); if (active) { // warp-level computation } __syncthreads(); // all threads must reach } ``` Actually wait, inside the `if (active)` block, there is no `__syncthreads()`. The shuffles are warp-level. So I can put the computation fully inside `if (active)` without issues as long as there are no `__syncthreads()` inside `if (active)`. Looking at the loop: ```cpp for (int tile_j ...) { // load smem (all threads) __syncthreads(); if (active) { for (int jj ...) { // computations using shuffles } } __syncthreads(); } ``` Yes, this works because all threads reach both __syncthreads(). **compute_dq_kernel:** ```cpp __global__ void compute_dq_kernel(...) { int b = blockIdx.x / H; int h = blockIdx.x % H; int kv_h = h / (H / H_kv); int row0 = blockIdx.y * ROWS_PER_BLOCK; int tid = threadIdx.x; int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int row = row0 + warp_id; bool active = (row < N); float q_reg[4], do_reg[4]; if (active) { const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = q + b*sqb + h*sqh + row*sqn; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = dout + b*sdob + h*sdoh + row*sdon; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; q_reg[i] = (d < D) ? __bfloat162float(q_ptr[d]) : 0.0f; do_reg[i] = (d < D) ? __bfloat162float(do_ptr[d]) : 0.0f; } } float row_m = 0, row_l = 1, row_D = 0; if (active && lane == 0) { int idx = (b * H + h) * N + row; row_m = m_ptr[idx]; row_l = l_ptr[idx]; row_D = D_ptr[idx]; } row_m = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_m, 0); row_l = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_l, 0); row_D = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, row_D, 0); float dq_acc[4] = {0,0,0,0}; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_K[BC][D+4]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_V[BC][D+4]; for (int tile_j = 0; tile_j < (N + BC - 1) / BC; ++tile_j) { int j0 = tile_j * BC; for (int idx = tid; idx < BC * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int jj = idx / D; int d = idx % D; const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = k + b*skb + kv_h*skh + (j0+jj)*skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = v + b*svb + kv_h*svh + (j0+jj)*svn; bool valid = (j0 + jj < N); smem_K[jj][d] = valid ? k_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_V[jj][d] = valid ? v_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } __syncthreads(); if (active) { for (int jj = 0; jj < BC; ++jj) { int j = j0 + jj; if (j >= N) break; float s_acc = 0, dov_acc = 0; #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; s_acc += q_reg[i] * __bfloat162float(smem_K[jj][d]); dov_acc += do_reg[i] * __bfloat162float(smem_V[jj][d]); } #pragma unroll for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { s_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, s_acc, offset); dov_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, dov_acc, offset); } float p = expf(s_acc * scale - row_m) / row_l; float ds = p * (dov_acc - row_D); #pragma unroll for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; dq_acc[i] += ds * __bfloat162float(smem_K[jj][d]); } } } __syncthreads(); } if (active) { __nv_bfloat16* dq_ptr = dq + b*sdqb + h*sdqh + row*sdqn; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; if (d < D) dq_ptr[d] = __float2bfloat16_rn(dq_acc[i]); } } } ``` Wait, I notice a subtle issue: `ds` is broadcast to all threads in the warp via the shuffle but each thread computes it locally. Since I did the shuffle reduction to lane 0, but then all lanes need `ds`. I need to broadcast it. Let me fix: after computing `s_acc` and `dov_acc` with warp reduction, I should have lane 0 compute `p` and `ds`, then broadcast `ds` to all lanes. Actually, in my code above, all lanes compute `s_acc` and `dov_acc` identically after the shuffle reduction (since shuffle XOR makes all lanes have the same sum). So all lanes will compute the same `p` and `ds`. That's fine! The shuffle XOR ensures all 32 lanes have the same value. So I don't need an explicit broadcast. Good. **compute_dk_dv_kernel:** ```cpp __global__ void compute_dk_dv_kernel(...) { int b = blockIdx.x / H_kv; int h_kv = blockIdx.x % H_kv; int col0 = blockIdx.y * BC_DKV; int tid = threadIdx.x; int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE; int lane = tid % WARP_SIZE; int col = col0 + warp_id; bool active = (col < N); float k_reg[4], v_reg[4]; if (active) { const __nv_bfloat16* k_ptr = k + b*skb + h_kv*skh + col*skn; const __nv_bfloat16* v_ptr = v + b*svb + h_kv*svh + col*svn; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; k_reg[i] = (d < D) ? __bfloat162float(k_ptr[d]) : 0.0f; v_reg[i] = (d < D) ? __bfloat162float(v_ptr[d]) : 0.0f; } } float dk_acc[4] = {0,0,0,0}; float dv_acc[4] = {0,0,0,0}; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_Q[BR_DKV][D+4]; __shared__ __nv_bfloat16 smem_dO[BR_DKV][D+4]; __shared__ float smem_m[BR_DKV]; __shared__ float smem_l[BR_DKV]; __shared__ float smem_Dstat[BR_DKV]; int rep = H / H_kv; for (int r = 0; r < rep; ++r) { int h_q = h_kv * rep + r; for (int tile_i = 0; tile_i < (N + BR_DKV - 1) / BR_DKV; ++tile_i) { int i0 = tile_i * BR_DKV; // Load Q, dO, stats for (int idx = tid; idx < BR_DKV * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int ii = idx / D; int d = idx % D; const __nv_bfloat16* q_ptr = q + b*sqb + h_q*sqh + (i0+ii)*sqn; const __nv_bfloat16* do_ptr = dout + b*sdob + h_q*sdoh + (i0+ii)*sdon; bool valid = (i0 + ii < N); smem_Q[ii][d] = valid ? q_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); smem_dO[ii][d] = valid ? do_ptr[d] : __float2bfloat16_rn(0.0f); } for (int idx = tid; idx < BR_DKV; idx += blockDim.x) { int ii = idx; int row = i0 + ii; int row_c = min(row, N - 1); int stat_idx = (b * H + h_q) * N + row_c; smem_m[ii] = m_ptr[stat_idx]; smem_l[ii] = l_ptr[stat_idx]; smem_Dstat[ii] = D_ptr[stat_idx]; } __syncthreads(); if (active) { for (int ii = 0; ii < BR_DKV; ++ii) { int i = i0 + ii; if (i >= N) break; float q_tile[4], do_tile[4]; #pragma unroll for (int i_d = 0; i_d < 4; ++i_d) { int d = lane * 4 + i_d; q_tile[i_d] = __bfloat162float(smem_Q[ii][d]); do_tile[i_d] = __bfloat162float(smem_dO[ii][d]); } float s_acc = 0, dov_acc = 0; #pragma unroll for (int i_d = 0; i_d < 4; ++i_d) { s_acc += q_tile[i_d] * k_reg[i_d]; dov_acc += do_tile[i_d] * v_reg[i_d]; } #pragma unroll for (int offset = 16; offset > 0; offset /= 2) { s_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, s_acc, offset); dov_acc += __shfl_xor_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, dov_acc, offset); } float m_i = smem_m[ii]; float l_i = smem_l[ii]; float D_i = smem_Dstat[ii]; float p = expf(s_acc * scale - m_i) / l_i; float ds = p * (dov_acc - D_i); #pragma unroll for (int i_d = 0; i_d < 4; ++i_d) { dk_acc[i_d] += ds * q_tile[i_d]; dv_acc[i_d] += p * do_tile[i_d]; } } } __syncthreads(); } } if (active) { __nv_bfloat16* dk_ptr = dk + b*sdkb + h_kv*sdkh + col*sdkn; __nv_bfloat16* dv_ptr = dv + b*svdb + h_kv*svdh + col*svdn; for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) { int d = lane * 4 + i; if (d < D) { dk_ptr[d] = __float2bfloat16_rn(dk_acc[i]); dv_ptr[d] = __float2bfloat16_rn(dv_acc[i]); } } } } ``` This looks good. Now let me write the full Python code. Actually, I need to handle the at::BFloat16 -> __nv_bfloat16 reinterpret cast carefully. Both are 16-bit values but they might not be layout-compatible in terms of type system. `reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat16*>(q.data_ptr<at::BFloat16>())` should work since both are just 16-bit values. Also, I need to add the proper includes and the pybind11 bindings via `load_inline`. One thing: `constexpr int D = 128` is fine. But I should also add `constexpr int D_PAD = 132` to avoid bank conflicts. Actually, for smem_K[BC][D_PAD], D_PAD=132 gives 132 * 2 = 264 bytes per row, not a multiple of 32... bank conflicts occur when multiple threads access the same bank. For bf16 (2 bytes), bank width is 4 bytes. To avoid conflicts for the warp loading a column, we might want D_PAD = 128 + some value. Actually, for `smem_K[jj][d]` where consecutive threads access consecutive `d`, this is coalesced and no bank conflicts. The issue is when warp threads access the same `d` index - which doesn't happen in our pattern. But when loading shared memory collaboratively, different threads might access different jj and d. In the loading loop: ```cpp for (int idx = tid; idx < BC * D; idx += blockDim.x) { int jj = idx / D; int d = idx % D; smem_K[jj][d] = ... } ``` This is sequential access, no bank conflicts. And in computation: ```cpp s_acc += q_reg[i] * __bfloat162float(smem_K[jj][lane * 4 + i]); ``` Here, each thread accesses `lane * 4 + i` which is consecutive across the warp. For smem_K[jj][d], each access is 2 bytes. Consecutive threads access addresses 2 bytes apart. With 32 banks of 4 bytes each, threads 0 and 1 access the same bank... Wait, this DOES cause bank conflicts! Let me think about shared memory banking: - Shared memory has 32 banks, each 4 bytes wide (on compute capability >= 3.x) - Consecutive 4-byte words are in consecutive banks - For bf16 (2 bytes), elements 0 and 1 are in bank 0, elements 2 and 3 in bank 1, etc. - Threads in a warp accessing smem_K[jj][lane * 4 + 0] where lane=0..31: - Thread 0 accesses bytes 0..1 -> bank 0 - Thread 1 accesses bytes 4..5 -> bank 1 - Thread 2 accesses bytes 8..9 -> bank 2 - ... - Thread 15 accesses bytes 60..61 -> bank 15 - Thread 16 accesses bytes 64..65 -> bank 0 (since 64/4 = 16, 16 % 32 = 0) Wait, thread 16 accesses bank 0 which conflicts with thread 0! But since each bank is 4 bytes and each access is 2 bytes, threads 0 and 16 are accessing different 2-byte halves of the same 4-byte bank. Depending on the GPU generation, this might be a 2-way bank conflict. To avoid this, we should pad D to 132 so that row jj starts at jj * 132 * 2 bytes. - Row 0: address 0 - Row 1: address 264 = 264 / 4 = 66 -> bank 2 (since 66 % 32 = 2) - Thread w accesses lane*4*2 = lane*8 bytes. That's 8 bytes per thread, 2 banks apart. Wait, lane*4 + i where i=0 means lanes access 0, 4, 8, 12, ... indices which are 0, 8, 16, 24, ... bytes. - Thread 0: bytes 0..1, bank 0 - Thread 1: bytes 8..9, bank 2 - Thread 2: bytes 16..17, bank 4 - ... - Thread 15: bytes 120..121, bank 30 - Thread 16: bytes 128..129, bank 0 (128/4 = 32 -> bank 0) CONFLICT! So with D=128, threads 0 and 16 access the same bank. With D_PAD=132, does it help? - Row starts at jj * 264 bytes - Thread w accesses jj*264 + (w*4+i)*2 bytes - For i=0: offset = jj*264 + w*8 - 264/4 = 66. 66 % 32 = 2. So row 0 starts at bank 0, row 1 at bank 2, row 2 at bank 4, etc. - But within a row, thread 16 still conflicts with thread 0 because 16*8 = 128, 128/4 = 32 -> 0. To completely avoid conflicts, we need D_PAD such that D_PAD * 2 is not a multiple of 128? Actually we need: for all lanes 0..31, (lane * 8) / 4 should be unique mod 32. But lane*8/4 = lane*2. lane*2 mod 32 for lanes 0..15 gives 0,2,4,...,30 (all unique). For lanes 16..31: 0,2,4,...,30 again. So there's inherently a 2-way bank conflict with 32 threads accessing 2 bytes each strided by 8 bytes. Wait, actually if threads access 2 bytes but the bank width is 4 bytes, then threads 0 and 1 accessing bytes 0..1 and 2..3 are in the SAME bank? No, bytes 0..3 are in bank 0. So threads 0 and 1 both access bank 0. But since they access different halves, some GPUs can broadcast this without conflict. Actually, the CUDA documentation says: "The shared memory hardware resolves bank conflicts for 32-bit and 64-bit accesses, but not for 8-bit and 16-bit accesses." For 16-bit accesses, any two threads accessing the same bank cause a conflict. For my access pattern `smem_K[jj][lane * 4 + i]`: - Thread lane accesses bytes (lane * 4 + i) * 2 = lane * 8 + i * 2 - For i=0: lane accesses byte lane*8 - Bank = (byte / 4) % 32 = (lane * 2) % 32 - For lane = 0..15: banks 0,2,4,...,30 - For lane = 16..31: banks 0,2,4,...,30 again So there are 2-way bank conflicts. For i=1: lane accesses byte lane*8 + 2 - Bank = (lane*8 + 2) / 4 % 32 = (lane*2 + 0) % 32 (integer division) Same 2-way conflict. So there's a 2-way bank conflict. This isn't terrible. In practice, shared memory bandwidth is high enough that 2-way conflicts don't hurt too much. For the dk_dv kernel, accesses to smem_Q[ii][lane*4+i] have the same pattern. To eliminate the conflict, I could use 64 threads per row (with 2 dims each) or use a different mapping. But 2-way conflicts are probably acceptable. Actually, I can have each thread handle 4 consecutive dims and access with a stride. Let me just accept the 2-way conflict and move on. It's not going to be the bottleneck. Now let me write the final code. I'll make sure there are no `__syncthreads()` inside conditional blocks and all static shared memory sizes are small enough. A `return` inside a kernel before `__syncthreads()` causes deadlock. Let me restructure: instead of `if (row >= N) return;`, I'll use: ```cpp bool active = (row < N); ``` And structure the computation so that all threads reach `__syncthreads()`.
